Krakow shooting suspect has died in hospital, police say

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

A gunman suspected of killing six people in central Krakow and his parents and sister at their home has died in hospital, Polish police say.

The 24-year-old was injured after opening fire in central Krakow, the second largest city in Poland, on Saturday, and died early on Monday morning, police said.

Armed officers raced to the scene and engaged in a gunfight with the suspect, who was shot within three minutes of the attack beginning, preventing a further massacre, officials said.

Officials initially reported the gunman had apparently been inspired by "far-right ideologies", but have since said they are still investigating any possible motivation.

After the shooting, which began shortly before 15:00 local time (14:00 GMT) in Krakow's busy Main Market Square, police identified the attacker as a 24-year-old Polish man. He has not yet been officially named.

In the hours after the shooting, a minister said early investigations indicate he was inspired by "far-right ideologies". The minister also described him as an "ultra-nationalist" without giving further details.

However, during a press briefing on Monday morning, PrzemysÅaw Nowak, a spokesperson for Poland's National Prosecutor's Office, said investigations into his motive remain ongoing.

Six people - four women and two men - are confirmed to have been killed in the attack.

People run for shelter as gunshots heard in Krakow

Four victims are Polish, while a British national and an Austrian woman were also killed.

Poland's Prime Minister Donald Tusk said three bodies - two women and one man - were found at the gunman's house when it was searched after the attack.

On Sunday, Polish police confirmed those three victims had been identified as the alleged gunman's 53-year-old father, 52-year-old mother and 21-year-old sister.

Polish media has reported the suspect had a firearms permit and may have obtained the weapons from his family home.

Four firearms were found in the gunman's car, including two AR-15 rifles and two handguns, along with several hundred rounds of ammunition, Nowak said. Authorities also seized at least four phones from his car and person, and electronic equipment from his home.

Sixteen people, including five foreigners, two police officers and the attacker, were taken to hospital. The interior ministry later said they were from Austria, Britain, Germany, Portugal and Russia.

Speaking on Sunday, one of two surgeons who treated victims on the street after the attack said his colleague "undoubtedly" saved the life of a gravely injured police officer.

The two surgeons, who were eating near the Main Market Square when the gunman opened fire, rushed to the scene to treat both the attacker and the two injured police officers.

Watch: Doctor who treated victims of Krakow shooting speaks to the BBC

Professor Michal PÄdziwiatr told the BBC: "We saw the shooting attack. And what a surgeon can do in a situation like this?... We decided to help the victims as soon as possible."

"We rushed into the area where the shooting was and started our rescue, because there were no ambulances available, there were no professionals around."

PÄdziwiatr said his colleague, who has been named by local media as Dr Mateusz Wierdak, used his hand to stem the bleeding from the most severely injured policeman, allowing time for an ambulance to arrive.

On Sunday it was reported that both injured officers had emerged from comas.

Witnesses who were near the Main Market Square have described their panic and confusion as the attack unfolded.

Ruby, from London, heard gunfire and "yelled at my friends to run and we all sprinted for our lives".

She said they "just kept sprinting" until they found a hotel to shelter inside.

Tom Brook, from West Yorkshire, was inside a McDonald's near the square when he heard "multiple gunshots and saw people running for cover".

"I then walked into the square and saw paramedics working on someone outside [a shop] and there was a dead body covered in foil blankets 20 meters away," he said.

In the wake of the shooting, Poland declared the country would enter two days of national mourning.

After travelling to Krakow, Tusk said: "Until now in Poland we haven't had such drastic cases of terrorism...that's what we have to call this cruel attack on people's lives here in Krakow."

UK Prime Minister Andy Burnham said he was "shocked and saddened by the appalling shooting in KrakÃ³w".

"My thoughts are with the families of those who have lost their lives, everyone injured and all those affected by this senseless violence. The United Kingdom stands in solidarity with the people of Poland," he added.

Main Market Square reopened on Sunday evening after a security cordon was lifted.

Wounded police officers 'saved many lives'

NASA keeps its PROMISE to raid Mars testbeds for parts

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Cut-and-shut trundlebot with spiffy new paint job meant to be headed to the Moon in 2028

NASA is moving ahead with its plan to repurpose a Mars rover engineering model as a lunar trundlebot for less than $400 million.

The robot, dubbed PROMISE (Polar Rover for Observation, Mapping, and In-Situ Exploration), is a cut-and-shut of engineering test articles used for the Perseverance and Curiosity rovers, currently rolling around the Red Planet.

During a weekend briefing, NASA outlined the plan for PROMISE: to land the robot on the lunar surface by late 2028 for an 18-month mission. PROMISE will be preceded by NASA's VIPER trundlebot, which is expected to survive only 100 days on the Moon's surface. PROMISE will carry copies of VIPER's science instruments, including a drill capable of boring 1 meter into the lunar regolith.

Power will come from a Radioisotope Thermoelectric Generator (RTG) left over from the Cassini mission. NASA acknowledged the fuel was processed some time ago – the Cassini mission to Saturn was launched in 1997 – but anticipates there will be plenty of heat and power for PROMISE's components.

Blue Origin is to deposit the rover on the Moon. NASA awarded the company a $94 million base contract, "with an option period worth an additional $140 million based on performance to deliver PROMISE to the lunar South Pole."

The figures make it difficult to ascertain the exact cost of the PROMISE mission. However, during the briefing, NASA said it had "a pretty firm agreement with JPL to basically deliver the rover to the surface under $400 million." The $400 million includes the lander, which will be Blue Origin's Mark 1.

Earlier this year, The Planetary Society's chief of space policy, Casey Drier, estimated that the mission (including repurposing the hardware developed for Mars and landing it on the Moon) would cost at least $723 million. At the time, NASA Administrator Jared Isaacman said: "if PROMISE costs even 20 percent of your low-end estimate, we simply will not fly it."

Assuming Isaacman was only talking about the rover, Drier's figure drops to $489 million without launch and landing costs. 20 percent of that is… not going to be enough.

That also doesn't account for the impact of taking away the hardware built for the Mars program. During the briefing, NASA said it was "pretty comfortable" with how the Mars rovers were going and so was happy about repurposing the hardware or, as one speaker put it, "can we give it a new paint job?"

A source told The Register that morale at the agency's Jet Propulsion Laboratory (JPL) was not high at present. Raiding successful programs to fulfill the administration's whims is unlikely to have improved the mood of engineers.

Still. Another trundlebot on the Moon. Far better than using that spare RTG for yet another deep space mission, right? ®

Bybit Expands RWA Earn Into Japanese Equities With New JPE Product

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Home » Crypto Market News » Bybit Expands RWA Earn Into Japanese Equities With New JPE Product

Bybit’s first equity product in RWA Earn gives access to an SBI Asset Management strategy.

Bybit is expanding its RWA Earn platform into Japanese equities with the launch of JPE, its first equity-based investment product.

Announced on November 9, the product marks the platform’s first equity offering and gives eligible users indirect exposure to a Japanese high-dividend equity strategy managed by SBI Asset Management (SBI AM).

The launch brings a new asset class to Bybit RWA Earn, which previously focused on fixed-income and credit strategies.

According to an official announcement, the JPE investment product will give eligible Bybit users indirect exposure to a Japanese high-dividend equity strategy managed by SBI Asset Management (SBI AM).

JPE is backed by JX, a digital token issued by DigiFT, a platform specializing in tokenized traditional investments.

Plume, a blockchain-based financial infrastructure provider, structures the product for distribution through Bybit’s RWA Earn platform. The underlying Japanese equity strategy remains managed by SBI Asset Management.

Bybit said eligible users can invest in JPE through its RWA Earn platform using USDC, with a minimum subscription of 5,000 USDC.

The exchange will cover all blockchain transaction fees, while subscriptions and redemptions will be free of charge. Redemptions will follow a T+5 settlement cycle, excluding non-trading days in Japan, Singapore and the United States.

Emily Bao, Bybit’s Head of Spot & Assets, said the launch reflects the growing role of partnerships in bringing traditional financial assets onto blockchain networks. She added that the collaboration with Plume and DigiFT allows Bybit to offer users access to institutional-grade investment products backed by established asset managers.

Full details, restricted regions and eligibility criteria are available in Bybit’s official announcement.

The launch marks an expansion of Bybit’s RWA offering into traditional equity markets.

Since its launch in June 2026, Bybit RWA Earn has focused on tokenized fixed-income and credit products, giving eligible users access to investment strategies beyond crypto-native assets.

With the addition of JPE, the platform is moving into equities for the first time, offering indirect exposure to a Japanese high-dividend equity strategy managed by SBI Asset Management.

The global real-world asset (RWA) market reached $39.61 billion in distributed asset value as of November 8, according to RWA.xyz, down 0.94% over the previous 30 days. The value of represented assets stood at $391.90 billion, up 8.87%. The market had around 5.69 million asset holders, an increase of 7.13% over the same period.

U.S. Treasury debt remained the largest RWA category, with $14.11 billion in distributed value, followed by commodities at $5.33 billion and active strategies at $4.72 billion. Tokenized stocks ranked among the larger categories, reaching approximately $3.73 billion.

Tokenized equities are moving from fixed income into stock-based strategies on Bybit exchange. It also gives eligible investors access to a Japanese high-dividend equity strategy through a blockchain-based product.

<i><em>Marshals</em></i> Is Ruining the Dutton Family Legacy

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

The Yellowstone spinoff wants to move on from its Kevin Costner-sized shadow, but it can’t.

Ever since John Dutton (Kevin Costner) died, the Yellowstone-verse has sought to do away with everything he stood for. Without the patriarch in charge, it’s dawned on his children, Kayce (Luke Grimes) and Beth (Kelly Reilly), that the man was too violent and too stubborn. So, Kayce sold the ranch and resolved to put his violent life behind in the spinoff, Marshals, while Beth moved to Texas in Dutton Ranch to try ranching her own way.

It’s not really working out. Beth and her husband Rip (Cole Hauser) basically just moved the whole Dutton operation to Texas. And as much as Kayce’s plan to turn his last remaining acres of the Yellowstone Ranch into an equine therapy center would follow through on his plan for peace, he’s too busy continuing his father’s cycle of violence by running into danger as a member of a team of U.S. Marshals every week. Why? Well, because peace for the Duttons just isn’t good TV.

Still, Marshals tries its best to weave the framework into season 2. The latest round of episodes to kick off the second season deals heavily with post-traumatic stress disorder (PTSD), and the various ways the Marshals team handles their crazy, violent lives. Mostly, it’s by bottling it up and never speaking about it. After killing a man in the season 1 finale, Kayce’s son Tate (Brecken Merrill) says a line in season 2, episode 3 that could have been plucked directly from a Dutton family quote book: “What happened stays where it is.”

But try as they might, the Dutton children are becoming their father anyway. By now in the task force spinoff, Kayce’s likely killed more men on the ranch than his father ever did. And though he’s not branding people like cattle anymore—which still haunts Kayce’s dreams in season 2, episode 4—even John Dutton gave up that practice after Yellowstone season 3.

Most likely, the Marshals team just wrote themselves into a trap. They might think they’re providing Kayce good backstory by tackling his inner quest for a peaceful life, but when he heads to work to kill criminals every morning, you can’t really believe the guy. Is he trying at all? And since he’s clearly not, maybe we should focus on that instead...?

So, at the end of episode 3, Kayce demands that Tate break his family’s generational cycle and take his ass to therapy. “It’s the last place my father would’ve taken me, and the last place I would’ve gone myself,” he says.

Two episodes have passed since that moment, and we haven’t seen Tate actually go yet. Plus, Kayce hasn’t exactly set a good example. Despite ordering his son to open up, Kayce didn’t give us any hints that he’s decided to see a therapist himself. And that equine center? We’ll be lucky if we ever see it until Marshals is over for good.

Egidio Vergani, taken from Campo Cecina, Carrara, Italy

LBN 182, a nebula in Cygnus, surrounds the Wolf-Rayet star WR 134 some 6,000 light-years away; the star’s powerful wind is blowing a faint bubble into the surrounding gas, visible at upper left as a bright blue shock front. Decades After the Go-Go’s Formed, Jane Wiedlin Finally Feels like a Role Model

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Jane Wiedlin credits discovering David Bowie’s music when she was 14 as setting her on her course as a musician. “If I hadn’t found him as a kid, I don’t know that I would’ve ended up discovering the punk rock movement, which was totally populated by ex-glitter-rocker kids,” she says. She attended his Diamond Dogs tour in 1974 and later opened for him as a member of the Go-Go’s on his Serious Moonlight Tour in 1983. Throughout her autobiography, TMI: Memoirs of a Go-Go, out Tuesday, she references the impact he had on her life. She says, “I can’t believe he’s dead because he really never seemed mortal to me.”

When he did die a decade ago, Wiedlin tells Rolling Stone she fell into a hole. “For a year after that, I didn’t listen to any music except Bowie — nothing,” she says. “Even after that year, I still felt heartbroken.”

At the time, his death coalesced with a number of other events that played into one of Wiedlin’s worst episodes of struggling with mental health, stories that lie at the core of TMI. Unlike many other books by famous musicians, this one never lingers long on Wiedlin’s musical victories, and even though she references her band’s name in the title, stories of the Go-Go’s make up less than half its contents.

Instead, she details her many rocky relationships, explains how the #MeToo movement changed her life, mourns the difficulties of albums like Cold (released with her band Frosted in 1996, during her sadly overlooked solo career), and restrains nothing about the time when she worked as what she calls a “junior dominatrix.” “Bi is best” is a philosophy she credits Bowie with teaching her. As a whole, the book is a self-portrait of not just Jane Wiedlin the musician, but Jane Wiedlin the person. “People just see me as a Go-Go,” she says. “They don’t know any differently. But if they read the book, they will discover I’m not just a Go-Go.”

Some of TMI can be difficult to read. She writes candidly about how she struggled with self-harm and suicidal ideation. But Wiedlin, 68, says she’s now in a better frame of mind. The book reads almost like a coming-of-age story about how she discovered who she was, a quality she hopes readers can relate to. Editor’s picks The 250 Greatest Albums of the 21st Century So Far The 100 Best TV Episodes of All Time The 500 Greatest Albums of All Time 100 Best Movies of the 21st Century

“I hope that reading my book will improve their lives in some way if they’re struggling with mental health stuff, if they’re secretly a kinkster, have been afraid to come out, or if they’re a young woman that wants to be in a band but is afraid,” Wiedlin says during a wide-ranging chat. “I think a lot of people could possibly benefit from reading it.”

Why was now the right time to write this book?I’ve thought about writing a book a lot over the years, and I was too scared because I didn’t want to hurt anyone. I knew that with my personality, I wasn’t going to be able to hold back; I was going to want to tell the whole truth. But on my 65th birthday, I started thinking about it again, and I thought, “If you don’t do it now, you don’t know what’s coming.”

Well, I know what’s coming is death, but before death, who knows what kind of stuff’s going to happen as far as my health and being an old lady and blah, blah, blah. So I just decided to take a chance and go for it.

In the end, I feel like I found my balance between being “TMI,” the title of the book, and just not throwing people under the bus or blaming other people for my mistakes and my problems. And once I took all the responsibility and put it on myself, the book became very easy to write.

Since you titled it TMI, did you ever feel like you had to hold back?I kind of barfed everything out, which is sort of how I always write. Writing the book was way easier than the editing … that was a bitch. A lot of people want to talk to me about the book, and honestly, it’s a little bit shocking. Related Content Runaways Songwriter Settles Sex Abuse Claims Against Kim Fowley, Still Pursuing Ex-KROQ DJ God Only Knows What We’d Be Without Brian Wilson See Billie Joe Armstrong Perform With the Go-Go's at Coachella Go-Go’s Guitarist Jane Wiedlin Claims Influential Rock DJ Sexually Abused Her as a Teen

Why are you shocked?I’m thrilled. Look at my career, man. After the Go-Go’s broke up in ’85, I kept working and making music, but I didn’t really have a career. I wasn’t making money at it. I feel like I became relatively invisible for decades, and the only time that I wasn’t was when I was with my band, the Go-Go’s. So when so many people were interested in this book and interested in me, I found it shocking.

When the Go-Go’s formed, you almost called the band the Misfits. Then the Kinks put out the Misfits album. When did you finally learn there was a punk band called the Misfits?Oh, my God, I didn’t hear them for ages after they got popular. They weren’t really on my radar because it wasn’t really my thing at the moment, but it is funny to think that we might’ve been the Misfits compared to what the actual Misfits ended up being.

Something that makes your memoir unusual is you don’t give a blow-by-blow story on the making of each of your albums, whether with the Go-Go’s or after. Why is that?There’d already been three books by other Go-Gos, and I didn’t want to go over any of the territory that they’d already covered. Also, it’s a book about my life and my life is just so much bigger than just my band.

When you quit the Go-Go’s you said you’d rather be a failure on your own than a success with the band.I did say that.

How do you feel about that now?Oh, God. I was an idiot to think that. That was a big, dramatic statement to make, and I had no idea what the fuck I was talking about. And as it turns out, it has been consistently difficult trying to be a solo artist as opposed to being a Go-Go. But I love making music. I love writing songs, so I just carry on and have almost gotten to the point where I have stopped caring what people think and just do it for myself.

The solo albums you made after the Go-Go’s are more pop than rock. Why did you go in that direction?I was very lost when I left the band. It was literally the only musical experience I’d ever had. I had no idea how to exist just on my own without having the support of bandmates.

Times were really different then. Record companies were super important. Now I don’t think it would matter if every record company folded today. Who gives a fuck, right?

Some of your songs then are great. I was listening to your first solo album today, and “Somebody’s Going to Get Into This House” strikes me as something St. Vincent might do now.That’s very flattering. Thank you. Maybe I should re-listen to my old stuff. There was a lot of painful moments during those years and fun ones, too, but really no one to share the fun with. It was hard, I was still young, and I was very lost.

Who did you lean on then?My first husband was already a music manager, so I think I leaned on him very heavily. He was my support system.

Did the other Go-Go’s tell you what they thought of your solo albums?None of us really ever talk about our solo work with each other. [Guitarist] Charlotte [Caffey] and I have written a lot on my solo stuff, so that’s sort of an exception to that statement. You’ve got to assume that there’s a certain amount of sibling rivalry between us, even though it’s not correct to think that we were like sisters because we were more like a really dysfunctional marriage with five women in it.

It has been interesting how we keep getting back together and then parting again and getting back together. No matter how much or how little we’re getting along or not getting along, they are always going to be my sister wives.

One of the aspects of your personality you write about a lot in the book is being a people pleaser, even going back to your childhood. How do you break out of that?I think over time, I’ve just realized the more bad stuff I go through, the bigger and the stronger I’ve become. And I’m so grateful for that, because I’ve been through some fucking shit that could have broken me.

I’ve really changed in the last couple of years. Writing the book is part of it because I examined my life — really examined it — and I wrote down my truth. It gave me more confidence.

You’re very open about your struggles with mental health. You felt suicidal from age 11 to 60. Did your family and friends know what you were going through?Absolutely not. The only time that it finally came to a head was in ’96. My husband was out at a business thing, and I was sitting in the house just sawing away at my arm, and I had never really cut like that before. It was really bad. I have a really gnarly scar from it. When he came home, I couldn’t hide it. He saw it, and he lost his mind, and then he was like, “You have to get help.” And that was when my journey to mental health basically started.

It’s up and down. I still had another fucking suicide attempt after that, but at least it’s been more up than down. Even with all my frailties and fears, I feel I am a stronger person today than I’ve ever been, and I’m not going back.

People are always like, “Oh, the Go-Go’s, they’re role models.” I feel like a role model now. It took me my whole fucking life to get to this point.

Good, you should. How should people support loved ones going through the things you went through?Parents need to support girl children. I think boy children get a ton of support. But it starts with making sure girl children know they can do anything and be anyone — the same things that they tell boys.

I think when girls hit puberty, it’s harder on them than boys. Parents need to be right there for them supporting them, because you go a little bit crazy when all those hormones change. Every girl does. It’s also encouraging girls to follow their bliss. If they like making art, support that. Help them do that. If they want to be a musician, get them instruments and lessons and play them music that you think will help them learn and grow.

It all seems kind of obvious to me, but first we had to have a crack in the system where we at least acknowledge the patriarchy, that things were not equitable, and that things need to change. I feel like we have acknowledged that even though we still meet resistance. I am very optimistic about the girls and women of the future.

You revisited the allegations of sexual misconduct you’ve made against Rodney Bingenheimer in the book. What struck me about what you wrote is you didn’t see what you describe as misconduct until the #MeToo movement. Why did it click then?I started hearing other women’s stories, and a very common thread was how much guilt we all felt because we didn’t say anything or fight back and that somehow made it our fault. To me, that was the penny dropping. Like, “Oh, my God. I never thought of it that way.” I still thought of it like, “Oh, it was my fault. I shouldn’t have let him do that.” And now I’m like, “Jesus Christ, I was 15.” I had barely kissed a boy at that point. It was so not my fault, so I shouldn’t have any guilt feelings about it. And you know what? I feel so much better about it now. [Bingenheimer did not reply to a request for comment.]

I was a fan of Frosted, your pop-punk band in the Nineties. You wrote about them as if nobody heard that album, Cold.Honestly, you’re a rare breed. There’s maybe 2,000 of you around the whole world, so you should feel special. I still love that record so much, and God, it was heartbreaking what happened with it. I don’t think I’ll really ever get over that, when you do something that you love so much and you believe in, and then it’s like, “Nope, next.”

One of your friends who worked for Frosted’s label, DGC, in the Nineties told you that the signing criteria for every artist they signed was, “Would Kim Gordon think this is cool?” Have you told Kim that anecdote?No, I’ve never even met Kim, but I will definitely bring it up if I do, because I would be very surprised if she knew about that. I think she would love to know because it’s just so fucking ridiculous. I mean, it is so cringe.

Since you couldn’t promote Cold at the time, let’s talk about it. What’s the story behind that album?I was full of ideas at the time and really turned on by the whole return of the punk movement. I couldn’t have been more happy the first time I heard Green Day on the radio.

Personally, I was struggling with my identity and my age because even back then, 100 years ago, I was starting to feel the pressure of aging. And I was mentally not in a good space. I was very depressed and I was cutting again and it was not a great time in my life, but always when I have those bad times, good songs come out of it, and that’s a consolation prize.

That Frosted album, and the one I made after it, Kissproof World, are very similar. Those are my favorite two albums I’ve ever made by far.

On those albums, you pair dark lyrics with poppy, upbeat music. Why did it come out that way?I started doing that in the Go-Go’s when Charlotte and I became writing partners. It’s always been my favorite songwriting trick. I’m as depressed as the next guy, but I don’t really enjoy listening to music that is not only depressing musically but also lyrically. It’s just a bit too much for my brain.

By the way, I appreciated how on the audiobook, you sing all the lyrics a cappella.Thank you. I had so many lyrics in the book that singing them a cappella was extremely scary. But I was like, “Fuck it, I’m doing this. I don’t fucking care.” Because why not? Otherwise I’d just speak them. Why not throw a little showbiz into it?

The album art for Kissproof World featured a lot of BDSM imagery, which, as you wrote in your book, you were living at the time. What are the most valuable life lessons you learned from being what you call a “junior dominatrix”?Well, first of all, power goes to the one that grabs it. Men do not necessarily have all the power, and many men want to give away their power, and they don’t want to be the one with it, at least temporarily. Also that men are really weird. I could write a book about all the weird things that men want and just pay for. I’m not talking about sex; I’m talking about just weird shit. So the human brain is very interesting, and I think the BDSM movement and lifestyle are like opening the doors to human brains. A lot of people don’t get it, don’t like it, and are grossed out by it, but I think it’s part of the human experience and it’s just possibly processing the world differently than vanilla people do.

You wrote a musical about Bettie Page but never produced it. What is its status now?I haven’t read that thing in 30 years or something. I have no idea if it’s any good or not. If I did want to do something with that, I would for sure want to do a major rewrite because I’m older and wiser now.

You wrote that you don’t see your BDSM work as sex. What is it, then?I don’t know. It’s something in between. It’s a power exchange for sure, because the submissive is giving up their power to the dom. It is exciting, and it is a fulfillment of fantasies. It is very physical sensation-based but also can be very mental as well.

If you look at the definition of sex, I don’t think that it would qualify. It’s not fucking, it’s not blowjobs, it’s not hand jobs, it’s not anal. It’s none of those things, right? But again, just because I don’t think it’s sex doesn’t mean that my husband wouldn’t have been really pissed off if he knew what I was getting up to.

Your whole life story seems to be about recognizing what you want in your life and going after it.Writing the book has changed my life for sure. Putting my thoughts down on paper and looking back, you’re like, “OK, so this led to this and that.” Until I wrote the book, I didn’t even make the connection that my first suicide attempt was shortly after I got molested by Rodney. I mean, that’s insane that I didn’t think of that. But after I wrote it all down, I was like, “Oh, my God, look at this.” Trending Stories Watch Ariana Grande, Ben Stiller, Robert De Niro Take Over ‘The Tonight Show’ U2 Sound Revitalized on ‘Carnaval de Luz’ Del Casher, Guitarist Who Invented the Wah-Wah Pedal, Dead at 88 Russell Brand Sentenced to More Than 10 Years in Prison After Rape Conviction

You have a solo album of cover songs called I Protest coming in the spring. What’s the state of the Go-Go’s now?We’re definitely not together as a five-piece, but we’re not broken up either. Belinda [Carlisle] works so much. I don’t know how she does it. She’s playing all over the world for two years. Gina [Schock] and I have done some shows lately together working with a backing band, and it’s been really fun. But as far as doing these big high-profile concerts with the five of us, it’s not really in the cards, at least not for the next few years.

The Go-Go’s were the first all-female band to get a Number One album by writing their own songs. What do you think needs to happen for another band to achieve the same thing?I have no fucking clue. It is such a miracle that the Go-Gos did what we did, and it is also very disappointing that it hasn’t happened again. I’m just going to hope that it will, it can, it has to happen again. But patriarchy still rules the world, and it definitely still rules the music business, so it’s not going to be easy. I hope someone goes for it and makes it.

Amanda Seyfried Reveals Her Daughter's Reaction to Mean Girls

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Amanda Seyfried shared her daughter Nina's reaction to watching Mean Girls for the first time during an exclusive E! News interview at the Steps world premiere in NYC.

Amanda Seyfried's fifth sense is knowing when to show her kids her work.

Although the Mean Girls alum and ex Thomas Sadoski's kids Nina, 9, and Thomas Jr., 6, aren't fully versed in her filmography, when it comes to what they have seen, they think she's pretty grool.

"They've seen the few animated movies I've done," Seyfried exclusively told E! News at the world premiere of her new Netflix movie Steps on Nov. 8. "My daughter watched Mean Girls for the first time last year. They love it."

Her kids know that she's not a regular mom, she's a cool mom.

"It's normal for them," the 40-year-old said of her career being in the spotlight. "I'm learning more every day. I think as they get older, they're like, 'Huh, what a very unique place to get to be.' I'm like, 'Yeah, it is unique!'"

And when it comes to expressing gratitude, for Seyfried, the limit does not exist.

Along with pressing play on the Lindsay Lohan-led movie—also starring Rachel McAdams, Tina Fey and Jonathan Bennett—her kids have also taken a chance on some of her other biggest hits.

The Dropout star—who announced her breakup from Sadoski in September after nine years of marriage—added, "They loved Mamma Mia."

Now, Nina and Thomas Jr. are watching the Oscar nominee find her happily ever after in Steps—premiering on Netflix Nov. 20—as Cinderella.

Although the animated movie is a reimagining of the fairy tale—in which the princess' stepsisters Lilith (Ali Wong) and Margot (Stephanie Hsu) are misunderstood, rather than evil, as they team up to save the kingdom from childhood bully Priscilla (Nikki Glaser)—it simply expands on the already beloved story.

"I saw her as somebody who was desperately searching for herself in some way," Seyfried said. "That hasn't changed. But I think we've really gone into this book, this fairy tale, this story that we all know, and they've completely upended it."

"It took the complexity of who we are as people in the world and turned it into this unique depiction of sisterhood and friendship," she continued, "and what it takes to fully be able to communicate and bond."

Seyfried will definitely be adding Steps to her kids' movie night queue. "It's beautiful," she beamed. "This is what I want my kids to be watching."

Granite Ridge: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Its operator partnerships secure Permian drilling locations for under $2.5 million each, a cost edge that shapes how durable the inventory growth really is.

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Nov. 6, 2026, Granite Ridge (GRNT +4.63%) reported that cash flow before working capital changes covered its drilling and completion capital for the quarter. Management expects adjusted free cash flow, defined as operating cash flow before working capital changes minus development capital expenditures, to fund future dividends and capital allocation.

Granite Ridge expands its inventory by partnering with private operators who source acreage through direct leasing rather than public auctions. This platform consistently secures Permian Basin drilling locations for less than $2.5 million per net location.

Natural gas pricing at the Waha hub, a West Texas regional market center, recovered from record low levels as new pipeline capacity opened. That recovery tripled realized natural gas prices sequentially and expanded total quarterly cash flow.

Granite Ridge established a clear framework for spending adjusted free cash flow as development capital spending catches up with cash generation. The Board authorized a $35 million share repurchase program while sustaining the company's quarterly dividend.

Production growth and higher oil weighting mark an operational turning point for the business. Granite Ridge maintains nonoperated positions across six basins with return targets above 25% at current commodity strip prices. Management is watching operational execution, cost inflation, and oil mix targets.

Management expects cash generation to strengthen further as production expands and legacy commodity hedges expire.

LGS Owners Continue To Debate Universes Beyond MTG Sets With Prerelease Figures

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Despite Magic: The Gathering's creator, Wizards of the Coast, insistingthat Universes Beyond is largely a well-supported and popular part of the game, it's getting hard to ignore the persistent negative narrative around these sets when prerelease comes around.

Star Trek officially hit prerelease last Friday, and while I enjoyed my own experience at my LGS, it's impossible to ignore the waves of disparaging comments online, whether it be about attendance, playability, or the fact that stores are reportedly being forced to buy Universes Beyond stock under threat of having in-universe stock limited if they don't.

Early online responses to the set and prerelease are bleak, and when you combine that with complaints from players who were willing to buy and invest in the set but couldn't get stock in some cases, it paints a picture of a poor end to 2026. The key, however, is that the negative voices are vocal online players and represent a minority of the game's audience, and this is becoming a consistent trend after the release of Universes Beyond sets that don't fit the typical Venn diagram of Magic players' presumed interests, because the silence after the release of The Hobbit was deafening.

The post that prompted my deeper look at how the Star Trek prerelease was performing overall came from Robert Taylor, in response to a call from Saffron Olive asking how the events were shaping up. The post, which is still live on Twitter / X, has Robert sharing attendance numbers for all of this year's pre-release events at their local store, Mayhem Comics in Iowa.

As you can see from the tweet above, Star Trek had the worst attendance of any event held at that store this year, with 62 attendees, while all in-universe sets had figures above 110. This is, of course, meaningless. It's one store's figure with zero context, but it's useful data when combined with the huge wave of other figures shared across social media and Reddit.

Players are reporting that Star Trek was the outright worst, or among the worst attended events of the year, with the common phrase I'm seeing in posts being "around half" when comparing Star Trek to Reality Fracture, Secrets of Strixhaven, and even The Hobbit.

Personally, my LGS has a capacity of 60, spread across two days: 40 on Friday and 20 on Saturday, while Marvel Super Heroes, Reality Fracture, and The Hobbit were sell-outs; Star Trek wasn't, but it wasn't poorly attended either. Four spaces remained for Friday and four for Saturday, so given the time of year and proximity to other sets, this isn't a bad result.

On the belief that store owners are being forced to overstock Universes Beyond products under threat that they will not receive any in-universe stock as a repercussion, the two WPN store owners who I spoke to deny this, with one going so far as to say they felt safe ordering less Star Trek stock based on customer interest and have been given no indications that their Nauctis stock is suffering as a result.

If you're getting ready to head to your LGS for Star Trek prerelease, then here's everything you need to know about the latest Universes Beyond set.

Wizards of the Coast won't reveal any stats or figures from the prerelease or launch of Star Trek unless they are positive, meaning we'll need to wait until the new year and the next financial update to find out what impact the set had, positive or negative, on sales figures.

Despite all the online bickering, a number of players and store owners believe they have identified the biggest reason Star Trek numbers are down in their areas: Reality Fracture was only six weeks ago.

Granted, there's an entire payday between the two sets, but given the current state of the world and Magic, players may have had to choose between the two sets and decided to put the larger chunk of their cash and time into the in-universe set.

We now have just over two months until the Nauctis: The Sunken Realm prerelease at the end of January, so players have the chance to rest and refresh, ready for a whole new year of Magic. With six sets confirmed on the MTG 2027 calendar, all of which are full-sized and launching with at least four Commander precons, let's just hope that production delays are something we leave behind in 2026, because that's certainly not helping how players are perceiving new sets either.

Amundi Boosts Strategy (MSTR) Stake 44% to $291 Million as Shares Slide

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Amundi, Europe’s largest asset manager, raised its stake in Strategy (MSTR) by about 44% in the third quarter, a Form 13F filed Nov. 6 shows, lifting the position to 1,903,725 shares worth $291.4 million at Sept. 30 prices.

That is up 579,672 shares from the 1,324,053 Amundi reported at June 30, when the same holding was valued at about $115.1 million. The Paris-based firm managed €2.579 trillion in assets at the end of September, up 11% year over year, and described itself as Europe’s leading asset manager.

The filing lands as MSTR has given back much of its third-quarter run. Strategy shares closed at $153.09 on Sept. 30 and near $126 on Monday, an 18% drop that would put Amundi’s disclosed stake near $239 million at the latest close, Yahoo Finance data show.

A 13F is a snapshot, not a trading diary. Amundi filed a combination report that folds in 17 other managers across its group, so the Strategy line can include client, fund and discretionary positions rather than a single proprietary bet, and the firm may have changed the holding since Sept. 30.

Strategy itself kept stacking. The company bought another 1,036 bitcoin last week for $81.9 million at an average of $79,012, a Monday 8-K filing and company release show, lifting its treasury to 850,364 BTC acquired for $64.16 billion at an average of $75,456.

That average cost now sits above the spot bitcoin price, leaving the stack underwater on paper even after the firm added 811 bitcoin the prior week. Strategy’s public ledger still ranks it as the largest corporate bitcoin holder, with the stack equal to roughly 4% of bitcoin’s 21 million supply cap.

MSTR roughly doubled during the third quarter itself. The June 30 13F valued Amundi’s then-smaller stake at an implied $86.93 a share, against the $153.09 Sept. 30 close, so the dollar value of the position rose faster than the share count. Since then the stock has tracked bitcoin’s slide into the mid-$70,000s, wiping out a large share of that mark-to-market gain for anyone still holding the Sept. 30 position.

The timing matters because Strategy funds most of its bitcoin buys by selling new MSTR common under an at-the-market program. A weaker share price raises more dilution for every coin purchased, which is the feedback loop that has defined the stock’s moves this year.

Amundi was not the only large European name adding bitcoin exposure on the quarter. Barclays’ own 13F, also filed Nov. 6, shows 3,942,900 shares of BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust (IBIT) worth $186.7 million at Sept. 30, up 48% from 2,658,147 shares three months earlier, SEC records show.

That Barclays line carries the same caveat: a 13F gathers client, managed and trading book positions, not proof the bank put its own capital into bitcoin. Still, the two filings together sketch the same picture of Q3: traditional European managers kept adding exposure to bitcoin proxies even as the spot market cooled into the fall.

The institutional adds sit against a weaker tape since quarter-end. Bitcoin has slipped into the mid-$70,000s, Strategy stock has followed it down, and the value of Amundi’s disclosed MSTR stake has compressed even as the share count on the filing stayed fixed. At Monday’s close the disclosed stake was about $52 million below its Sept. 30 mark.

Whether Amundi kept buying, trimmed, or simply held through October’s slide will not show up until the next 13F. For now the Nov. 6 filing is the record Europe’s biggest asset manager left: nearly 1.9 million Strategy shares, up 44%, at a $291 million quarter-end mark.

Southwest Airlines Offers “Free” Same-Day Flight Changes, But There’s A Seat-Fee Trap

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Southwest Airlines offers eligible passengers free same-day flight changes, but there is a catch if you paid separately for your seat. Asking an airport agent to make the change can leave that seat purchase nonrefundable, while making the change through the app, website, or telephone receives different treatment. Apparently, how you change your flight now matters almost as much as which flight you choose.

View From The Wing highlights a peculiar provision in Southwest’s seat refund and exchange policy.

For passengers who separately purchased a seat on their original flight, Southwest explicitly states:

“Same-day change requested at the airport: Seat purchased on original flight is non-refundable.”

But complete that same-day change through Southwest.com, the Southwest app, or by telephone, and the rules provide a way to apply the original seat payment toward a replacement or receive a refund when buying a more expensive seat.

You could be standing at the gate with your phone in hand, looking at an earlier departure. Whether you tap the screen or ask the employee in front of you for help could affect what happens to money you already spent.

The policy concerns seats purchased separately from the airfare. Here is how Southwest describes the treatment when you voluntarily switch flights:

For a hypothetical example, suppose you paid $40 for a seat and make a confirmed same-day change through the app. If the replacement seat costs $30, Southwest applies the original payment but does not return the $10 difference.

If the replacement costs $50, you pay $50 upfront and generally receive a separate $40 refund. You should therefore check that the refund actually arrives rather than assuming the system simply charged you the difference.

Under Southwest’s same-day change policy, the confirmed-change benefit is available with Choice, Choice Preferred, and Choice Extra fares, along with Getaways by Southwest vacation bookings.

You need an available seat on a flight between the same origin and destination airports on the same travel date. The change must occur at least 10 minutes before your original scheduled departure.

Basic passengers must upgrade to an eligible fare first (and A-List or A-List Preferred status alone does not make a Basic ticket eligible for a free confirmed same-day change)

So when it comes to standby, there are two separate things to check: whether you can switch flights without an additional airfare charge, and what happens to the seat you purchased.

Do not confuse a confirmed change with same-day standby.

A confirmed change secures your place on the replacement flight immediately. Standby means waiting for space to become available, often much closer to boarding or even after boarding commences if you are waiting for no-shows. Southwest’s seat policy says the original seat purchase becomes nonrefundable when standby clears, regardless of how you joined the list.

Using the app therefore does not solve the seat-payment issue for standby. The eligibility rules differ too. A-List and A-List Preferred members can qualify for standby on Basic fares, even though those fares do not qualify for free confirmed same-day changes.

Of course, switching flights does not guarantee the same seat. Someone who bought extra legroom on one departure cannot reasonably expect an identical seat to materialize on a replacement flight. But why should the channel used to request the change determine the treatment of the original payment?

If Southwest can apply or refund that purchase when I use its app, why should asking an airport employee produce a less favorable rule? There may be a systems explanation, but that does little for a passenger who reasonably assumes an agent can provide the same options available online. On the other hand, I would bet that an agent who assigns you a superior seat in these sorts of situations is not going to charge for it…any frequent Southwest travelers want to weigh in?

This also puts employees in an awkward position. A passenger asks for help, receives it, then discovers that handling the transaction another way could have preserved the value of a seat purchase. For an airline that built so much goodwill around simplicity, this seems like an unnecessarily complicated way to handle a relatively ordinary request.

If you paid separately for a Southwest seat and want to make a confirmed same-day flight change, check the app or website before asking an airport agent to process it. Telephone changes also receive the more favorable treatment outlined in the policy.

Compare the replacement seat cost and keep track of any refund due. If you are using standby, remember that the app does not preserve the refundability of your original seat purchase once you clear.

A free flight change can still have consequences for the extras you purchased. Southwest passengers now have another reason to read the fine print before accepting what sounds like a straightforward benefit.

SpaceX now fundamentally an "infrastructure company," senior official says

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

“Grok analyzing the same video is 99.9 percent perfect.”

For a long time, SpaceX has been synonymous with the development of newer, larger, and ever-more-reusable rockets. But that began to change over the last decade with the introduction of Starlink, and the change has accelerated during the last 18 months as the company has pivoted to data and artificial intelligence.

At last week’s UP.Summit conference in Florida, the vice president of launch at SpaceX, Kiko Dontchev, offered some of the most revealing comments to date about the company’s changing focus, how it is embracing AI, and why the Falcon 9 rocket—which has dominated global launch for a decade and remains the world’s preeminent launch vehicle—is firmly on the road to retirement.

“In the last couple years, when I talk about SpaceX, yes, we are a rocket company, but fundamentally we are an infrastructure company at this point,” Dontchev said. “We’re building infrastructure on the ground and low Earth orbit, soon on the Moon, and obviously eventually Mars.”

Earlier this year, SpaceX acquired one of founder Elon Musk’s other companies, xAI. SpaceX engineers were already using xAI’s tools, Dontchev said, but in recent months, employees have really started to figure out how artificial intelligence can speed up their work.

“What’s happened at SpaceX is we’ve really gone through a huge acceleration of adoption,” Dontchev said. “Even just a year ago, we weren’t adopting it that quickly, but really starting this year, we started to adopt it extremely rapidly. And the acceleration we have seen in our ability to do engineering is unlike anything I’ve ever seen at the company in my 16 years.”

Tasks that would take SpaceX employees hours to days now take seconds to minutes, he said. And it’s not just the engineers. Dontchev said the work of identifying foreign object debris, or FOD, in aerospace parts has been largely automated using the AI assistant Grok. This debris can cause catastrophic damage to engines and other spacecraft components.

“We’ve automated being able to detect FOD,” he said. “We’ll have an inspector go in and borescope a tube, and we find that humans are about 94 percent perfect (at) finding a piece of it. Grok analyzing the same video is 99.9 percent perfect.”

SpaceX has also automated visual inspections of other hardware it produces, such as Starlink customer terminals, due to similar gains in accuracy. Dontchev said AI is now being used at every level of the company’s operations.

“We’ve truly started to integrate it to where every single person, including the technicians and the technician leaders, are starting to use it in their day-to-day, and it’s completely leveled the playing field and accelerated people’s growth,” he said.

Since SpaceX’s founding in 2002, a community of spaceflight enthusiasts gravitated to the company because of Musk’s vision for humanity becoming a multiplanetary species, beginning with the settlement of Mars. But concern has grown in this group that SpaceX is drifting away from that vision. Musk has recently made a hard pivot toward the deployment of orbital data centers, envisioning hundreds of thousands of solar-powered satellites providing computing services to users on Earth. Musk has largely stopped talking about sending humans to Mars, except in passing.

Dontchev insisted that human settlement and Mars remain part of the core of SpaceX’s aspirations.

“We have come to understand that this tool is critical to us accelerating our pace of innovation,” he said of artificial intelligence. “Without AI, I think we still would have gotten to the Moon. I think we still get to Mars with SI. We’re going to get there so much faster.”

Dontchev arrived at SpaceX just before the company flew its first Falcon 9 rocket in mid-2010. But because the company was never able to solve second-stage reuse, SpaceX needs to move on, he said.

“Falcon 9, I love that rocket to heart,” he said. “I’ve launched many of them, all of them, in fact. But it’s still not fully reusable. We still throw that second stage away every single flight. Starship—we’re going to catch both the first stage and the second stage.”

For 100 launches a year, it is possible to fly a partially reusable rocket, he said. But for a company that aspires to launch thousands of rockets a year, and eventually maybe even 10,000, the cost and time lost in manufacturing a new second stage for every launch is simply not sustainable.

It is also significant that Dontchev spoke less about Starship’s payload capacity and more about its productivity per launch. The first orbital Starship launch, in late September, carried larger Starlink V3 satellites. And in the aggregate, this single mission carried 10 times the bandwidth of the Starlink payload launched by a Falcon 9.

Flying Starship from Florida, he said, will further improve capacity compared to Texas launches. Launching from Florida offers more favorable trajectories to certain orbits, potentially allowing Starship to carry more satellites without expending propellant on dogleg maneuvers. Dontchev said a single Florida Starship launch will be able to carry up to 20 times the bandwidth of a single Falcon 9.

To that end, the pad infrastructure for Starship’s first launch tower at Kennedy Space Center’s Launch Complex 39A is nearly finished. The company is also developing two additional pads in Florida.

“Pad 39A is effectively complete,” he said. “We’re going to transport boosters and ships over barge here to the Cape. We’re hoping to actually do the first transports this year in December, and then maybe even fly by the end of the year.”

Starship is clearly being optimized to serve SpaceX’s launch needs. But what about commercial launch customers that have relied on Falcon 9 rockets over the last decade?

During his remarks, Dontchev did not address widespread concerns in the US commercial space industry and beyond that SpaceX has not moved to replace the Falcon 9’s lost capacity with Starship. For now, multiple sources have indicated, SpaceX is holding Starship launches in the coming years for its own needs, and potentially those of NASA and other government customers.

With our unique combination of technical savvy and wide-ranging interest in the technological arts and sciences, Ars is the trusted source in a sea of information. After all, you don’t need to know everything, only what’s important.

Is This Pete Hegseth’s Last Stand?

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Secretary of Defense Pete Hegseth and his “patriotic cowboys” at the Pentagon used to warn America’s adversaries fuck around and find out. After Tuesday’s election, it looks like Hegseth is the one who is going to find out.

Sources on Capitol Hill and in military circles think Hegseth’s time at the Pentagon is coming to an end with Rep. Thomas Massie, a lame duck Kentucky Republican, planning to introduce articles of impeachment this week. But defense officials are cautioning that the embattled defense secretary has survived numerous missteps.

In response to calls for his impeachment and firing, Hegseth launched a coordinated PR campaign with a short video and photos across Pentagon social media channels of Hegseth with “STAND. FIGHT. DEFEND THE REPUBLIC.” tagline. Chief Pentagon Spokesman Sean Parnell says Hegseth won’t be deterred from executing President Trump’s agenda in the face of “sham witch hunts” and “phony investigations.” Parnell says Hegseth is committed to leading the Defense Department until the end of Trump’s term.

So far, Trump is supporting Hegseth, telling reporters the secretary is doing a good job, but reports before the election said the president asked advisers whether Hegseth should stay.

“It looks like his days are very seriously numbered,” James R. Webb, a Marine Iraq combat veteran who worked as a military legislative assistant for Sen. Rand Paul, tells me. “If they keep him, he’s probably going to be the first guy impeached by the new Congress.”

The threat of impeachment had been building before the election and could grow now that Democrats hold strong majorities in both chambers. In September, Massie introduced articles of impeachment that accuse Hegseth of violating the War Powers Resolution, ignoring laws meant to minimize civilian casualties, and carrying out extrajudicial killings in the boat-strike campaign against suspected drug traffickers, which left at least 221 dead.  Massie told CNN last week the only thing that would prevent him from introducing articles of impeachment when Congress reconvenes is Hegseth’s resignation.  Editor’s picks The 250 Greatest Albums of the 21st Century So Far The 100 Best TV Episodes of All Time The 500 Greatest Albums of All Time 100 Best Movies of the 21st Century

One rumored soft landing for Hegseth was Marsha Blackburn’s Senate seat, now vacant after her victory in the Tennessee governor race. But that was shot down when Jake Sherman of Punchbowl News reported Hegseth wasn’t being considered, which didn’t surprise Webb.

“He has basically burned every bridge possible with people on the Hill,” Webb says. “They don’t like him.”

With no apparent chance at Tennessee’s vacant Senate seat, Hegseth appears ready to fight, comparing himself to Trump in the almost-six-minute video Thursday.

“It’s all about the politics of personal destruction,” Hegseth says about Massie’s articles of impeachment. “Didn’t work against President Trump, and it won’t work with me.”

A former senior defense official who worked with Hegseth calls the video — which featured tough talk, culture war tropes, and a defense of the war in Iran — “bonkers” after Tuesday’s resounding defeat of MAGA, but said it likely resonated with only Trump, which is all that matters.

“He is sending the signal that he’s a tough guy and going to keep fighting,” the former senior defense official says, joking that it sounds like Hegseth used AI to write the speech. “Hey ChatGPT, what are the things that I can say that are most related to the history of MAGA,” the former senior defense official jokes. Related Content Russell Brand Sentenced to More Than 10 Years in Prison After Rape Conviction Houston Mayor Limits Cooperation With ICE: ‘Elections Matter’ Macklemore Says Nine U.S. Venues Turned Down ‘Free Palestine’ Tour: ‘They Got Me F-cked Up’ Trump’s First Big Post-Midterms Move Is Promoting Stephen Miller

But the move is straight from the Roy Cohn playbook. The lawyer who sat at Joe McCarthy’s elbow during the Red Scare and later taught a young Trump how to fight. Admit nothing. Deny everything. Never apologize. And no matter what, claim victory.

It isn’t surprising that the video is tone deaf. Hegseth’s echo chamber of online trolls and yes-men insulates him from the professional officer class that nicknamed him “No Tab Pete” after he failed to earn his Ranger qualification as an infantry officer. One former senior officer tells me Hegseth would never rate high enough to serve in the military’s most elite formations, despite his fascination with special operations.

As the swirl around Hegseth increases, sources say there’s a sense of anticipation. “Folks across the force are listening and waiting,” a senior officer tells me.

While there is optimism that Hegseth might finally be gone, some of my sources — current and former officers and defense officials — didn’t want to get their hopes up after two years of hearing about his pending removal.

“He has an audience or one, and the president stands behind him,” the former senior defense official says. “If you were asking me to put $100 bucks on it, I’d say he’s staying.”

But if Hegseth leaves, some will celebrate because being forced to follow a Fox News weekend host into war strained even the most professional soldiers. Hegseth’s two years at the Pentagon divided the ranks. At first, savvy generals and leaders — particularly in the Army — used the political cover to enact real reforms around procurement and preparing the force for a modern battlefield. But when Hegseth set his sights on them, the very embodiment of the warrior ethos Hegseth worships — generals like Christopher Donahue, who served with America’s most elite units and led the transformation of the Army based on lessons learned in Ukraine — were chopped, seemingly for political reasons. The senior officer says well-qualified officers — both male and female — are taking their names off promotion lists because they don’t want to work closely with the current leadership.

“Those removed were warfighters,” the senior officer says. “More officers than I can count said that it is so much harder to be good at our jobs [under Hegseth].”   Trending Stories Watch Ariana Grande, Ben Stiller, Robert De Niro Take Over ‘The Tonight Show’ U2 Sound Revitalized on ‘Carnaval de Luz’ Del Casher, Guitarist Who Invented the Wah-Wah Pedal, Dead at 88 Russell Brand Sentenced to More Than 10 Years in Prison After Rape Conviction

The senior officer hopes the next defense secretary will bring more professionalism to the Pentagon, with less emphasis on clicks and likes on social media. Most of the people I talked with laughed when Ron DeSantis’ name was floated as a replacement last week. Many expect the former Florida governor to run for the White House in 2028, and tying his reputation to a lame-duck Trump administration would be political suicide.

Most senior leaders tied to this administration watched their political futures disintegrate on Tuesday. Leaving Hegseth to rant from the Pentagon steps as the reckoning comes. Or as Retired Maj. Richard Ojeda, the newly elected democratic congressman from North Carolina representing Fort Bragg, put it on X: “Tick tock, No Tab Pete.”

Disney and Adobe are building generative AI tools together

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

The partnership will give Disney ‘early access’ to AI tech for its resorts, cruise line, ESPN graphics, and more.﻿

The partnership will give Disney ‘early access’ to AI tech for its resorts, cruise line, ESPN graphics, and more.﻿

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

The Walt Disney Company has teamed up with Adobe to develop proprietary “artist-led” generative AI models to support Disney’s production pipeline. The multi-year partnership was announced today at Adobe’s MAX conference, and will see Adobe providing Disney with early access to its latest technologies for everything from Disney’s theme parks and cruise line to ESPN content. Disney and Adobe will also collaborate “to explore emerging technologies, including responsibly developed AI, according to Adobe’s press release:

“With Adobe Firefly Foundry, Disney will work directly with Adobe on generative AI models tailored to Disney’s properties that are human-centered and artist-led. Customized on Disney properties, these proprietary models are trained on unique world knowledge (character attributes, environments, visual styles) to generate commercially safe and production-ready outputs, while accelerating work and enabling the development of more creative projects.”

The announcement builds on the existing relationship between the two companies, with Adobe noting that Disney’s Imagineering R&D division is already using its Firefly AI platform for theme park development. Adobe will contribute to “expand internal creative workflows” at Walt Disney Studios, and identify how AI can be used to shape ESPN content and “enhance the engagement with sports fans.”

This is the latest example of Disney embracing generative AI tools, following a previous deal with OpenAI and involvement with Character.AI’s bankruptcy. Bloomberg reported last week that Disney is gearing up to lay off thousands of staffers and targeting 20 percent annual cost savings, in what’s expected to be its fourth round of job cuts in 2026.

Toronto couple claim landlord is evicting them for “farting too much”

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

A Canadian couple and comedy duo are fighting to keep their home after claiming their landlord is trying to evict them over their excessive farting.

S.E. Grummett and Sam Kruger, who perform together as Creepy Boys, have found themselves in a bizarre dispute involving an air-quality monitor at their apartment in Toronto.

The pair claim their landlord believes they’re smoking indoors, causing unusual readings on the device. However, they insist the actual explanation could be something much more embarrassing. Now, they’re raising money to fight their eviction.

In a GoFundMe campaign, the couple explained that the trouble began in December 2025, when their landlord, who lives upstairs, noticed elevated levels of potentially harmful substances in the air.

The two apartments share ventilation, and their landlord was concerned about the readings because of a recently diagnosed lung condition.

Grummett and Kruger said they tried everything from avoiding cooking to showering elsewhere, but the readings continued to rise.

After researching the issue, they discovered that the monitor could be reacting to normal bodily functions, including breathing and, as it turns out, flatulence.

“We are being wrongfully evicted because we fart too much,” they wrote on their fundraiser. “Really. This is not a joke. We wish we were joking.”

However, their landlord reportedly continued to suspect indoor smoking, which the couple denies. They were eventually served an eviction notice stating that the landlord wanted to reclaim their apartment for personal use.

“We believe this to be in bad faith,” they added. “In Ontario, such evictions have to be heard by the Landlord Tenant Board. So, we are going to court.”

The dispute has since reached Ontario’s Landlord and Tenant Board, where a decision remains pending, according to IBTimes.

Meanwhile, the couple’s fundraiser has surpassed its initial CAD $2,800 target, raising more than CAD $3,400 towards legal expenses.

Paul Anka, Stevie Wonder, Cher & More Kennedy Center Honorees Who Were Teenagers When They First Hit the Billboard Hot 100 (Full List)

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Most of the artists on this list weren't teen idols, as such. They simply knew what they wanted to do from an early age and got off to a fast start.

Paul Anka was just 15 – two weeks away from turning 16 – when he first made Billboard’s pop chart in July 1957 with the smash “Diana.” (This was more than a year before Billboard introduced the Hot 100.) Last week, Anka was announced as a 2026 recipient of the Kennedy Center Honors, one of the most prestigious career cappers in the entertainment field.

Anka is the 11th artist who later received the Kennedy Center Honors who first hit the Hot 100 as a teenager. Anka was a bona-fide teen idol when he started out, with such teen-targeted hits as “Put Your Head on My Shoulder” and “Puppy Love,” but most of the artists on this list weren’t teen idols, as such. They simply knew what they wanted to do from an early age and got off to a fast start.

In addition to these 11 artists who received Kennedy Center Honors as individuals, two artists who received the Honors as part of a group first made the Hot 100 as teenagers. Pete Townshend was 19 when The Who first made the Hot 100 with “I Can’t Explain” in March 1965. The Who received the Kennedy Center Honors in 2008. Verdine White was 19 when Earth, Wind & Fire first made the Hot 100 with “Love Is Life” in July 1971. EWF received the Kennedy Center Honors in 2019.

This year’s other Kennedy Center Honorees are songwriter/producer David Foster, actor/director Andy Garcia, playwright David Mamet, and country star Barbara Mandrell. The awards will be presented on Dec. 7 at Capitol One Arena in Washington, D.C.

Here’s a complete list of artists who went on to receive the Kennedy Center Honors who first hit the Hot 100 as teenagers. They are listed in chronological order by the year they received the Kennedy Center Honors.

Year Received Kennedy Center Honors: 1994

Year Received Kennedy Center Honors: 1999

First pre-Hot 100 hit: “Hey, Schoolgirl” (Tom & Jerry)

Year Received Kennedy Center Honors: 2002

First Hot 100 hit: “Bad Girl” (The Miracles)

Year Received Kennedy Center Honors: 2006

First Hot 100 hit: “Your Heart Belongs to Me” (The Supremes)

Year Received Kennedy Center Honors: 2007

First Hot 100 hit: “Surfin’” (The Beach Boys)

Year Received Kennedy Center Honors: 2007

First Hot 100 hit: “Why? Am I Treated So Bad” (The Staple Singers)

Year Received Kennedy Center Honors: 2016

Year Received Kennedy Center Honors: 2017

First Hot 100 hit: “All I Really Want to Do”

Year Received Kennedy Center Honors: 2018

Notes:  Cher’s recording of the Bob Dylan song hit the Hot 100 one week before Sonny & Cher notched their first hit, “I Got You Babe.”

First Hot 100 hit: “Every Beat of My Heart” (Pips)

Year Received Kennedy Center Honors: 2022

Year Received Kennedy Center Honors: 2026

Russell Brand Victim Says When She Told Channel 4 Managers About Assault ‘It Was Brushed Under the Carpet’

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

One of Russell Brand’s victims says when she told Channel 4 she had been assaulted by the comedian during a shoot for the network, senior management “brushed [it] under the carpet.”

Brand was sentenced to 10 years and six months in prison on Monday after being convicted of one count of rape and three counts of sexual assault last week.

During the sentencing hearing the victims submitted statements about how the assaults had impacted their lives. Related Stories NFL to Face New Trial in Sunday Ticket Class-Action Lawsuit to Determine Financial Damages

One victim, who in 2009 was ordered to give Brand a massage before the pushed his face into her groin and verbally abused her during a shoot, said she had reported the assault to Channel 4 but nothing was done.

“Immediately after the incident I spoke up, and sadly it was brushed under the carpet by senior management,” she said in her statement, which was read out by a lawyer for the Crown Prosecution Service. “This really affected me and had something similar happened again at that time, I know I would not have had the confidence to speak up again. There was a culture at the time which meant I felt I had to just carry on and not talk about it anymore.”

The CEO of Channel 4 at the time was Andy Duncan, who stepped down in Nov. 2009. In 2023 the network’s documentary strand “Dispatches” broadcast the result of a joint investigation with The Sunday Times about Brand in which a number of women alleged they had been sexually assaulted by the comedian. None of the alleged victims were involved in the recent court hearing although one is now believed to be assisting with a further police investigation into other incidents.

She said the assault has continued to impact her life. “Following the incident with Rusell Brand I was really upset. I was wary and on my toes about something similar happening again,” she said in the statement. “I felt I lost my trust in people and felt that my working environment wasn’t safe. From that point onwards I made sure that I wasn’t sitting in a room alone and consciously made sure I had a colleague with me in a professional environment.”

In his sentencing remarks, the judge, Justice Bennathan, referred to the incident, saying it was an example of Brand’s “staggering arrogance” and showed how his “entourage indulged your dreadful behaviour.”

“[The victim] reluctantly pressed her thumbs into your shoulders, then exclaimed how bony you were, leading your acolytes to laugh (possibly at you rather than her), and you to leap up and start insulting her with childish but sexualised comments,” the judge told Brand before handing down the sentence.

The two members of Brand’s entourage accused of ordering the victim to give the massage were Jack Bayles and Gareth Roy. Bayles, who is head of comedy at “I May Destroy You” producer Various Artists Limited, announced following last week’s verdict he would be stepping back from his role until further notice.

In a statement, he said he couldn’t remember the incident but added: “I would never willingly or proactively wish to make anyone feel uncomfortable, unsafe or unsupported at work. If I was ever present when Brand acted inappropriately and I failed to spot it and step in, then I sincerely apologise.”

Variety has contacted Channel 4 for comment.

Street Fighter Officially Removed From Over 1,000 Theaters After Box Office Drop

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Street Fighter is losing its fighting spirit on the big screen.

Street Fighter is one of the most popular video game franchises of all time, particularly when the focus turns to action releases. There have been multiple Street Fighter adaptations over the years, including both live-action and animated movies and TV shows. In terms of live-action films, the franchise first came to theaters through the release of 1994's Street Fighter, which featured names like Jean-Claude Van Damme as Guile and Ming-Na Wen as Chun-Li. The franchise tried to focus on the latter character through a solo film in 2009's Street Fighter: The Legend of Chun-Li, before 2026 brought forth a new take on the franchise.

Now, the new Street Fighter movie is dealing with the loss of 1065 theaters on its fourth domestic weekend (via Box Office Mojo) alongside a box office drop. 2026's Street Fighter started its domestic wide release in a whopping 3575 theaters on October 16. While it is natural for a movie to lose theaters throughout its run, the film had already been removed from 750 theaters by its second weekend, likely as a result of Street Fighter's $30.7 million domestic opening weekend, which came in lower than expected for a video game adaptation that reportedly had a budget of $82 million.

Based on that budget, Street Fighter needs to finish its box office run between $164 million and $205 million to break even, which is becoming hard with a loss of another 1065 theaters and a global box office total of $99.1 million at the time of writing. Street Fighter's new massive loss of theaters coincides with a brutal box office drop for the video game adaptation. The action film brought in just $2.1 million in its fourth and latest domestic opening weekend. That pushed all the way down to outside the Top 10 Movies at the domestic box office for November 6-8.

Street Fighter ended up at #11 on the chart, with several new theatrical arrivals helping push it out of the coveted domestic top 10. One of those happens to be the movie that dominated the list, the live-action sci-fi sequel Godzilla Minus Zero, which broke records with a $31.6 million debut, making it the highest-grossing foreign-language film debut at the domestic box office in history. Fierce competition and the fact that Street Fighter's reviews have not been kind to the movie, as the action-packed release holds a low 42% critics' score based on 146 reviews, likely impacted its performance.

In ScreenRant's Street Fighter review, Brandon Zachary gave the movie a 7 out of 10 score, noting how Street Fighter always picks style over substance throughout its 119-minute runtime. Street Fighter features many beloved characters from the video game franchise, with actors like Noah Centineo as Ken, Andrew Koji as Ryu, Callina Liang as Chun-Li, Cody Rhodes as Guile, Jason Momoa as Blanka, and many more. Street Fighter focuses on Chun-Li recruiting Ken and Ryu to take the fight to the evil M. Bison, played by David Dastmalchian, as they enter a fighting tournament.

Schiff: Saylor's STRC Is 'Dead'

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Earlier today, gold bug Peter Schiff claimed that Strategy executive chairman Michael Saylor was trying to prop up the floundering investment product by carrying out heavy stock buybacks.

"Michael, STRC is dead. You have been keeping it alive on artificial life support with your buybacks," he wrote.

According to Schiff, Strategy is running out of money to prop up its preferred stock. The ongoing Bitcoin market crash is going to aggravate the situation even further, he argued.

"But you're running out of cash. And the selling pressure on STRC will increase as Bitcoin crashes," Schiff wrote.

The Bitcoin bear went on to criticize the generous 12% annualized dividend yield of STRC, saying that ultimately the yield wouldn't be enough to cover the risks of the product.

"It won't be nearly enough yield to cover the risk. 12%," he warned.

The stern comments from Schiff followed a typical bullish tweet from Saylor. In his post, Saylor claimed that AI could not mint new Bitcoin.

"AI can make me rap. It can't print Bitcoin," the cofounder of Strategy wrote.

Strategy's Variable Rate Series A Perpetual Stretch Preferred Stock (STRC) seeks to provide regular dividend income to investors while trading near its $100 face value.

Currently, STRC offers a 12% annual dividend yield, which Strategy has been working to keep steady in order to keep the stock trading near its face value.

However, maintaining the price stability of STRC has come at a big cost.

In its Q3 financial results on Oct. 29, Strategy reported that the firm had spent a total of $2.04 billion on repurchases of STRC as of Oct. 25.

Describing the heavy buybacks as a success, the firm said it had successfully put STRC on the road to financial recovery.

However, these buybacks have come under fire from Schiff, who questions whether the firm can keep propping up investor demand by repurchasing its shares forever.

In spite of the bearish comments from Schiff, Strategy isn't budging on its strategy of ramping up its Bitcoin holdings.

The firm's recent regulatory filing shows that Strategy bought another 1,036 BTC between Nov. 2 and Nov. 8.

The total cost of these purchases is estimated to be $81.9 million, with an average purchase price of $79,012 per BTC.

With the latest purchase, Strategy now holds an eye-watering 850,364 BTC.
